19, 2010, beginning at 7:00 PM in the Services Building, 4420 Livernois, Troy, MI 48098.

listed below. Items do not have to be taken in the order shown on this meeting notice. 
 
Unless removed from the consent agenda, items identified within the consent agenda will be 
acted on at one time.

      2. C.   01.19.10 
 
B.  Recognition      
 
 
1.   Board of Education Recognition 
 
Each January the Troy School District recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very important group of individuals: The Board of 
Education. Troy is truly fortunate to be governed by Board members 
who are tireless, professional, and passionate about improving the 
opportunities of our students. The successes that we receive in the 
Troy School District stem in large part from the support and guidance 
we receive from our Board members. The Troy Board of Education 
works very hard to ensure that the entire district is committed to 
promoting student achievement. Tonight, we would like to honor the 
Board of Education with a short slide show that features thanks and 
some advice from Boulan Park Middle School students.

RECOGNITION 
Reading Recovery Council of 
Michigan Award 
(Davisson) 
 
President Underwood called the meeting to order at 
7:00 p.m. 
 
 
In addition to Ms. Underwood, present were Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, and Philippart.    
Mr. Miletti and Mrs. Pochodylo were absent.  Also 
present were Superintendent Fowler; Administrators 
Adamczyk, Davisson, McAvoy, and Stromayer; and 
approximately 15 additional members of the audience. 
 
 
The Board recognized Mrs. Kathleen Davisson, 
Assistant Superintendent-Elementary Instruction, who 
was the 2009 winner of the prestigious Reading 
Recovery Council of Michigan Award.   
 
ADDITIONS AND DELETIONS 
 
 
None.

President, addressed the Board of Education regarding 
school funding, teacher of the year nomination forms, 
and she extended an invitation to the Board of 
Education to meet with the TEA Executive Board. 
 
Mr. Bruce Bloomingdale addressed the Board about 
school finance and the importance of teachers.

Comments  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
It was moved by Mrs. Edmunds and supported by Mrs. 
Philippart that be it therefore resolved  that the Board 
of Education takes action to schedule a Special Board 
Workshop on Tuesday, January 12, 2010, at the 
Administrative Center at 6:00 p.m. 
 
Ayes: 5 
Nays: 0 
Absent:    Miletti and Pochodylo 
 
Enxhi Xhafa, student representative from Athens 
High School, reported that next week Athens will be 
participating in a canned food and clothing drive and 
their goal is to collect 7,654 cans. 
 
Mrs. Fleming reminded everyone that the Troy 
Foundation for Educational Excellence will hold their 6 th  
annual train show at Athens High School on Sunday, 
January 19, 2010.  The show will run from 10:00 a.m. 
to 3:00 p.m. and tickets are $3.00.  All proceeds go to 
the Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence.  Mrs. 
Fleming also wished everyone a happy holiday. 
 
Mrs. Philippart asked Dr. Fowler to explain what is 
going on in Lansing regarding school funding and the 
“Race to the Top” legislation.     
 
Dr. Fowler said that she had spoken with Judy Allen, 
our lobbyist, who said that nothing will be done in 
Lansing before the holiday.  Legislators are looking at 
getting the bills out of the house and senate by the 
third week in December, but she does not hold much 
hope for a final vote prior to the end of the year.  Judy 
also said that there is not much chance of the 
12
Board of Education Meeting 3 December 1, 2009 
legislators taking up any of the other issues such as the 
$127 per pupil reduction or 20j funding until after the 
first of the year.  Dr. Fowler mentioned that district 
administrators and parents are planning to go to 
Lansing on Wednesday, December 9, 2009, to speak 
with Representative Marty Knollenberg and Senator 
John Pappageorge.  Anyone interested in going with Dr. 
Fowler and the group to Lansing should contact Tim 
McAvoy, director of community relations.  They will 
leave from Central Office at 9:30 a.m. and return at 
approximately 3:00 p.m. 
 
President Underwood thanked everyone for attending 
the meeting and wished everyone a happy and healthy 
holiday season. 
 
The next regular meeting of the Board of Education will 
be Tuesday, January 19, 2010. 
 
ADJOURNMENT 
 
It was moved by Mrs. Fleming and supported by Mrs. 
Philippart that the meeting be adjourned. 
 
Ayes: 5 
Nays: 0 
Absent:    Miletti and Pochodylo 
 
The meeting was adjourned at 7:57 p.m. 
 
 Respectfully submitted, 
 
 
 
 
 Nancy L. Philippart, Secretary

Race to the Top (RTTT) 
Memorandum of Understanding 
(MOU) 
10-059 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
None 
 
None 
 
None 
 
 
It was moved by Mrs. Philippart and seconded by Mrs. 
Pochodylo that: 
 
WHEREAS, the American Recovery and 
Reinvestment Act of 2009 (ARRA) provides, among other 
things, 4.35 billion dollars for the Race to the Top Fund, a 
competitive grant program designed to encourage and 
reward states that create the conditions for education, 
reform and significant improvements in student 
outcomes. 
 
 
 
 
      5. D.        01.19.10  
17
Special Board of Education Meeting 2 January 11, 2010 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 WHEREAS,  as a condition to applying for and 
receiving Race to the Top funds, the State of Michigan is 
required to adopt a final statewide plan in conformity with 
ARRA requirements, a condition that has not been met as 
of the date the Board adopted this resolution. 
 
 WHEREAS,  as a condition to applying for and 
receiving Race to the Top Funds, the Troy School District is 
required to execute the Michigan Participating LEA/ISD 
Memorandum of Understanding, attached hereto, which 
incorporates the requirements of the yet-to-be 
promulgated final statewide plan. 
 
 WHEREAS, the State Superintendent, Michael 
Flanagan, has represented, in writing, he will permit 
school districts, upon request, to rescind their agreement 
to the attached Michigan Participating LEA/ISD 
Memorandum of Understanding; 
 
 WHEREAS,  regardless of the State of Michigan’s 
failure to timely promulgate a final statewide plan as 
required by the ARRA, the Troy School District is required 
to submit its application for Race to the Top Funds on or 
before the January 7, 2010 deadline; 
 
 NOW, THEREFORE, IT IS RESOLVED:   That the 
Board President and the District’s Superintendent are 
authorized to sign the Michigan Participating LEA/ISD 
Memorandum of Understanding attached hereto. 
 
 IT IS FURTHER RESOLVED:   That the District’s 
Superintendent, after receiving direction from the Board 
of Education, is authorized to rescind the District’s 
agreement to the attached Michigan Participating LEA/ISD 
Memorandum of Understanding if, as written or 
interpreted, it incorporates terms or conditions that are 
not in the best interest of the District or the District’s 
students. 
 
Discussion of the motion followed.  It was then moved by 
Mrs. Philippart and seconded by Mr. Miletti that the 
resolution be amended as follows: 
 
 
 
18
Special Board of Education Meeting 3 January 11, 2010 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
AMENDED RESOLUTION 
 
WHEREAS, the American Recovery and 
Reinvestment Act of 2009 (ARRA) provides, among other 
things, 4.35 billion dollars for the Race to the Top Fund, a 
competitive grant program designed to encourage and
 
reward states that create the conditions for education, 
reform and significant improvements in student 
outcomes. 
 
 WHEREAS,  as a condition to applying for and 
receiving Race to the Top funds, the State of Michigan is 
required to adopt a final statewide plan in conformity with 
ARRA requirements, a condition that has not been met as 
of the date the Board adopted this resolution. 
 
 WHEREAS,  as a condition to applying for and 
receiving Race to the Top Funds, the Troy School District is 
required to execute the Michigan Participating LEA/ISD 
Memorandum of Understanding, attached hereto, which 
incorporates the requirements of the yet-to-be 
promulgated final statewide plan. 
 
 WHEREAS, the State Superintendent, Michael 
Flanagan, has represented, in writing, he will permit 
school districts, upon request, to rescind their agreement 
to the attached Michigan Participating LEA/ISD 
Memorandum of Understanding; 
 
 WHEREAS,  regardless of the State of Michigan’s 
failure to timely promulgate a final statewide plan as 
required by the ARRA, the Troy School District is required 
to submit its application for Race to the Top Funds on or 
before the January 7
 12 , 2010 deadline; 
 
WHEREAS , the Troy School District and its Board 
do not authorize and do not accept or approve, the 
proposed amendments by the State of Michigan or its 
Department of Education to Section III, new paragraph 
numbers III(6) and III(7) of the original MOU distributed by 
Michigan Department of Education before January 6, 
2010, nor authorize execution of any document that may 
be alleged or construed to include provisions appearing in 
the updated MOU distributed on January 6, 2010 that did 
not appear in the original MOU. 
19
Special Board of Education Meeting 4 January 11, 2010 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
WHEREAS , the Troy School District’s Board of 
Education is replacing Section V of the original MOU, as 
stated below: 
 
V.  DURATION/TERMINATION 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding shall be 
effective beginning with the date of the last 
signature hereon and, if a grant is received, ending 
upon the expiration of the grant project period, or 
if the grant is not received, ending on the date the 
grant is denied, or upon mutual agreement of the 
parties, whichever occurs first. 
 
Notwithstanding anything to the contrary, it is 
further agreed as follows: 
 
1)  The Participating LEA/ISD shall have the right to 
rescind this Memorandum of Understanding and 
not participate in the Race to the Top Grant Project 
until such time as the Board has had a reasonable 
opportunity to review the State’s final Application 
and final Scope of Work and fully considers the 
effect and ramifications of its participation in the 
Project. 
2)  The execution of this Memorandum of 
Understanding by the Participating LEA/ISD shall 
not constitute a waiver of any of its rights under 
the Headlee Amendment (Article 9, Section 29 of 
the Michigan constitution of 1963) or other 
applicable state, federal or local laws. 
 NOW, THEREFORE, IT IS RESOLVED:   That the 
Board President and the District’s Superintendent are 
authorized to sign the amended  Michigan Participating 
LEA/ISD Memorandum of Understanding attached hereto, 
with the understanding that no additional provisions shall 
be added to the Memorandum without the consent of the 
Board. 
 
 
IT IS FURTHER RESOLVED:   That the District’s 
Superintendent, after receiving direction from the Board 
of Education, is authorized to rescind the District’s

team to represent the Board.

7.   Recognition      
 
   B. NASA Downlink 
 
The Troy School District has been selected as Michigan’s 
only community – and one of only six in the nation – to host 
a live downlink with crew members of the International 
Space Station.  This is truly a community event and the 
experiences for our students will go well beyond the 
morning of February 1.  I know when you see this 
presentation you will get a sense of the energy level of the 
team that is putting a tremendous amount of work to make 
this event a success. To begin the presentation, I would like 
to introduce Jennifer Gottlieb, the Troy School District’s 
Science and Math Coordinator.  
 
 
Recommended action:  Receive the report.

7. CURRICULUM 
 
 C. CONSIDERATION:  Great Start Readiness Grant Acc eptance  
 
 The Troy School District is eligible to received funds from the Michigan Department of Education to 
provide educational services for at-risk 4-year-old preschoolers.  To accept the funds, the Board is 
required to approve a resolution certifying that the Troy School District will participate in the 2009-
2010 Great Start Readiness State Aid program and to indicate the type of services we will provide. 
 
 
                 Moved by:           
 
              Supported by:     
 
 
WHEREAS, the Legislature continues to acknowledge the importance of improving school readiness 
by providing quality preschool education for four-year-old children who may have extraordinary 
need of special assistance and the importance of providing parenting education programs for 
families of all children, birth to kindergarten entry; and 
 
WHEREAS, Sections 32d and 37 through 40 of the State School Aid Act provide funding for 
comprehensive compensatory education for these children and families; and 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with the provisions of Section 38 of the State School Aid Act, Troy School 
District is eligible to receive and allocation of $54,400 for fiscal year 2009-2010 to operate a school 
readiness program for 16 children and/or to provide comprehensive parent involvement and 
education programs for all families of children, birth to kindergarten entry; now therefore be it 
 
RESOLVED that Troy School District certifies that it will not provide full-day preschool education for 
eligible four-year-old children and that $ -0- of the stated amount, based on the approved per-capita 
allowance of $6,600 will be used specifically for this purpose; and therefore be it 
 
RESOLVED that Troy School District certifies that it will not provide comprehensive parent 
involvement and education programs for all families of children, birth to kindergarten entry, and that 
$ -0- will be used specifically for this purpose; now there be it resolved 
 
RESOLVED that Troy School District certifies that it will provide half-day preschool education for 16 
eligible four-year-old children and that $54,400 of the stated amount, based on the approved per-
capita allowance of $3,400, will be specifically for this purpose; and 
 
Furthermore, the Troy School District certifies that more than fifty percent of the children enrolled in 
the school readiness program will meet the income eligibility guidelines outlined in Sections 32d and 
37 through 40; and be if finally 
 
RESOLVED that any unallocated funds, as a result of this resolution, shall be redistributed to other 
eligible districts. 
 
Adopted this 19 th  day of January 2010. 
 
 
Ayes:              
 
Nays:

D. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105  
 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school distr icts are 
required to pass a resolution each school year indi cating 
whether or not they plan to implement a Schools of Choice 
program for nonresident students who attend another  
district within their county.  It would be appropri ate at this 
time for the Board of Education to consider adoptin g a 
resolution regarding Schools of Choice within Oakla nd 
County. 
 
 
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
 
 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 
 Moved by: __________________________  
 
 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
 
 
be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy 
School District, exercising the option permitted by  Section 105 of the 
State School Aid Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, 
will accept applications for a limited Schools of C hoice program for 
the 2010-2011 school year for nonresident kindergar ten and first-
grade students within Oakland County.

the consolidation of services agreement with the Mi chigan 
Department of Education.

 Moved by:       __________________________ 
 
 Supported by:  __________________________ 
 
 WHEREAS , MCL 388.1611D(2) requires that a district enter 
into an agreement with the Michigan Department of Education (MDE) 
no later than February 1, 2010 if the district inte nds to apply the 
$165 per pupil deduction in 2009-10 State School Ai d revenue 
against one of the unprotected State School Aid cat egoricals other 
than Section 22b; and 
 
 WHEREAS , the Troy School District intends to continue to 
offer the programs for which it currently receives categorical funding; 
and 
 
 WHEREAS , the district has already implemented cost savings  
through cooperative purchasing and service consolid ation initiatives; 
and 
 
WHEREAS , the district continues to pursue any and all 
consolidation of services that make sense for the district without such 
agreement; and 
 
266
 8. A.           1.19.10  
 
 
 
WHEREAS  the district intends to continue to realize saving s 
from cooperative agreements and service consolidati on plans 
currently in place; and 
 
WHEREAS , the district is unclear of what consequences woul d 
accrue if, despite our agreement to study specific service 
consolidations, we found that they would not yield financial savings;  
 
 NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT the Troy 
School District will not enter into an agreement wi th the Michigan 
Department of Education to develop a Service Consol idation Plan 
under section 388.1611D(2) of the Michigan Compiled Laws.

 B. CONSIDERATION:  Media Center Books  
 
  Invitations to bid were sent to 11 potential bidd ers with 8 
responding (one of which was “No Bid”.)  The bids w ere 
reviewed by Mr. Scott Bryan, Director of Technology, and it 
is recommended that the bid be awarded as stated below.   
 
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION :  Acceptance of the resolution below. 
 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
 
Be it therefore resolved that the bid for Media Center Books be 
awarded to Follett in the amount of $36,157.54 and that this amount 
be approved as an expenditure of the 2004 Capital Projects Fund.   
 
 
Ayes: ____________ 
 
Nays: ____________ 
272
 DATE:  January 14, 2010 
  
 TO: Michael Adamczyk 
  Assistant Superintendent 
  Business Services 
   
 FROM: Frank E. Lams 
  Supervisor, Purchasing and Budget 
 
          RE:      Bid Recommendation #9615 
                      Media Books                      
 
 
 
On July 15, 2009 bids were opened for a three year contract to supply media center 
books for all district buildings.  This bid was sent to 11 companies with eight responding, 
one of which was a “No Bid.” 
 
Results of this bid have been reviewed with Mr. Scott Bryan, Director of Technology, 
along with various Media Specialists.  This bid requested specific pricing on a 
representative sample of titles along with percentage off catalog pricing.   
     
Based on the results of these prices along with guaranteed titles offered, it was 
determined that, in the best interest of the District, a primary and secondary vendor 
would be utilized when purchasing books.  Follett was identified as the primary vendor 
for secondary school use. 
 
According, I recommend award of $36,157.54 in media books to Follett as an 
expenditure of the 2004 Capital Projects Fund.  Please include this as an action item on 
the January 19, 2010 Board agenda.

Department Department Department Department    
 
1140 Rankin 
Troy, MI 48083

  An Equal Opportunity/Affirmative Action Employer

To: Frank Lams, Purchasing Supervisor 
From: Scott Bryan 
Re: Board Action – IA East media purchase 
Date: January 7, 2010 
Cc: Michael Adamczyk, Assistant Superintendent, Business Services 
 
Dear Frank, 
 
International Academy East has submitted an order to complete the startup of their school library, 
following the process outlined by the District’s media book purchasing process.   
 
Follett was identified as the vendor of first choice for middle and high school purchasing in this 
competitively bid process, therefore the purchase meets Board of Education and State of Michigan 
bidding requirements.  The total cost of the books is $36,157.54.  This request is to approve the 
purchase of $36,157.54 worth of library books, a total of 1408 books, 1 audio-visual title, and the 
corresponding processing to allow cataloging of the titles into our system. 
 
Mr. Adamczyk has approved this amount from capital projects, so IA East will require a bond account 
number to place the order if it receives final approval.  This item requires Board action due to the 
dollar amount of the purchase; please place this on the January 19, 2010 agenda.  Thank you for your 
attention to this matter. 
 
 
 
 
Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D., Superintendent of Schools
